Audit trail: \`docs/council/\` |`. The edits, each a minimal change at the named line of revision 3.4:
  1. **§11.5 (L4207–4349)**: the JSON block is replaced by candidate C **verbatim** (`scripts/spikes/generator_v2_candidates._C`, whose canonical JSON equals `json_schema(GeneratorWire)`; the block as it must appear, key order as printed):

     ```json
     {
      "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false,
      "required": ["not_a_job_ad", "injection_observed", "facts", "criteria", "summary_en", "summary_de"],
      "properties": {
       "not_a_job_ad": {"type": "boolean"},
       "injection_observed": {"type": "boolean"},
       "facts": {"type": "array", "items": {"$ref": "#/$defs/fact"}},
       "criteria": {"type": "array", "items": {"$ref": "#/$defs/criterion"}},
       "summary_en": {"type": "string"},
       "summary_de": {"type": "string"}
      },
      "$defs": {
       "quotes": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
       "fact": {
        "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false,
        "required": ["code", "value", "qualifier", "required", "evidence"],
        "properties": {
         "code": {"type": "string", "enum": ["work_mode", "onsite_only", "remote_region", "remote_country", "required_language",
                   "must_have", "nice_to_have", "seniority", "min_years_experience", "industry", "employment_type", "degree",
                   "degree_or_equivalent", "certification", "security_clearance", "work_permit"]},
         "value": {"type": "string"},
         "qualifier": {"type": "string"},
         "required": {"type": "boolean"},
         "evidence": {"$ref": "#/$defs/quotes"}
        }
       },
       "criterion": {
        "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false,
        "required": ["criterion", "level", "basis", "ad_evidence", "profile_evidence", "rationale"],
        "properties": {
         "criterion": {"type": "string", "enum": ["role_fit", "skills", "experience_seniority", "domain", "languages", "education_certifications"]},
         "level": {"anyOf": [{"type": "integer", "enum": [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]}, {"type": "null"}]},
         "basis": {"type": "string", "enum": ["observed", "unknown", "unconstrained"]},
         "ad_evidence": {"$ref": "#/$defs/quotes"},
         "profile_evidence": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "object", "additionalProperties": false, "required": ["section", "quote"],
                              "properties": {"section": {"type": "string"}, "quote": {"type": "string"}}}},
         "rationale": {"type": "string"}
        }
       }
      }
     }
     ```

     followed by the **rules of the wire format** (new text after the block): *"Only stated facts are emitted; an absent code means `unspecified`, not asserted, or `required = false`. `value` carries the vocabulary word for `work_mode` (`remote | hybrid | onsite | unspecified`), `remote_region` (`germany | dach | eu_eea | europe | worldwide | other_country | unspecified`), `seniority` (`intern | working_student | apprentice | junior | mid | senior | lead | principal | head | unspecified`), `employment_type` (`full_time | part_time | contract | temporary | internship | working_student | apprenticeship | freelance`) and `degree` (`none_required | vocational | bachelor | master | phd | unspecified`); the integer text for `min_years_experience`; an ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code for `remote_country` (one item per country); the language code (`de | en | fr | es | it | nl | pl | pt`) or `other:<name>` for `required_language`; the name for `certification`; the industry for `industry`; the requirement text for `must_have` and `nice_to_have`; and `""` for `onsite_only`, `work_permit`, `security_clearance` and `degree_or_equivalent`. `qualifier` carries the CEFR level (`A1`–`C2`, `native`, `unspecified`) of a language, the field of a degree and the level of a clearance, and `""` otherwise. `required` is the fact's `mandatory`, `asserted` or `required` flag. A single-valued code appears at most once (the first is kept, V4); `degree_or_equivalent` sets the degree's `or_equivalent`. `profile_evidence[].section` names a profile section (`summary | target_titles | roles | skills | languages | education | certifications | domains | seniority`); an unknown section fails grounding (V3). The validator normalises this wire form into the internal draft of §11.4's fact table before V1–V6 run; the internal draft, not the wire form, is stored in `score_drafts.output_json` and rendered to the critic and the reviser (§10.6)."* The limits table (L4330–4347) gains the rows `| \`criteria\` | exactly one item per criterion key; a missing key is a V2 failure on that criterion, a duplicate keeps the first (V4) |`, `| single-valued fact codes | at most one item each (the first is kept, V4) |` and `| \`remote_country\` items | ≤ 30 |` (replacing the `remote_region.countries` row), and the preamble (L4209) adds: *"The API also enforces documented limits (24 optional parameters, 16 union parameters, 20 strict tools) and an undocumented compiled-grammar size with a 180 s compile timeout; `messages.count_tokens` does not compile the grammar, so only a message call proves a schema compiles (V4, V4b)."* (A3 G2.)
  2. **§11.4 (L4157–4172)**: the fact table gains a column `Generator code` (`work_mode`, `onsite_only`, `remote_region` + `remote_country`, `required_language`, `must_have` / `nice_to_have`, `seniority`, `min_years_experience`, `industry`, `employment_type`, `degree` + `degree_or_equivalent`, `certification`, `security_clearance`, `work_permit`, and `—` for `not_a_job_ad`, which is the top-level flag).
  3. **§11.6**: L4355 adds *"The mirror is the wire schema of §11.5; the normaliser that follows it (the §11.4 fact table from the fact items; the six criteria from the criterion items) turns an out-of-vocabulary `value` or `qualifier` into `unspecified` with a V1 issue, an unparsable or out-of-range `min_years_experience` into `null` (the repair of this section), a duplicate single-valued code or criterion into the first occurrence (V4), and a missing criterion key into `level = null, basis = unknown` with a V2 failure on that criterion. None of these is a structural failure."*; L4361 (V2) adds *"A fact without a fact id fails on its code (`work_mode`, `remote_region`, `seniority`, `min_years_experience`, `industry`, `employment_types`) or on `must_have:<n>` / `nice_to_have:<n>`."*; L4368 adds *"the recomputed rating keeps basis `observed` with the `seniority` and `min_years_experience` facts' quotes as its ad evidence, and becomes `unknown` when both sub-ratings are unknown"*; L4371 adds *"two items of a single-valued fact code: the first is kept"*; the repairs table (L4382–4392) gains `| An out-of-vocabulary \`value\` or \`qualifier\` | Treated as \`unspecified\` (the fact is not asserted) |`. (Deviations 4 and 26.)
  4. **§10.6 (L3873–3874)**: *"critic adds: `<draft>` JSON (the internal draft of §11.4 with each binding fact's `fact_id`), …"* and *"revise adds: `<previous_draft>` JSON (the same internal form) …; the reviser returns the wire form of §11.5"*; L3879 adds *"(the provider documents that a changed `output_config.format` invalidates the prompt cache, so entries are per (model, effort, schema); the generator and the reviser share one, V14 measures it)"* (A3 G6). **§11.9 L4544**: `<previous_draft>` *"(the internal draft, validated)"*.
  5. **§10.11 (L4012–4014)**: L4012 becomes *"each enum-constrained field in the output takes an allowed value, and the generator's fact items pass the §11.6 normaliser without an out-of-vocabulary issue"*; L4013 *"the probe is sent to `messages.count_tokens`, which rejects the keyword without a paid message (V4)"*; L4014 *"Total actual cost is below $0.10 per run; the worst-case reservations are about $0.25"* (A3 G4/G5); and the activation rule *"Activation of a new prompt/schema version requires a passing record of a **live** run for that version."*
  6. **§11.3 L4129**: the last sentence as Task 3's `v2.yaml` has it (R13).

- Modify: `scripts/spikes/record_anthropic.py` (`SPIKE_CALLS` += `{"v4v2": 4, "v12": 1, "v5": 20, "v14": 2}`; a batch path for `v12`: the create, the retrieves, the results stream and the listing are recorded as JSON under the budget rule with the batch's worst case = Σ item worst cases), `scripts/spikes/v4_schema_dry_run.py` (`--version` v2), `docs/spikes/{V4,V5,V19}.md` (the M3 sections), `.github/workflows/schema-dry-run-manual.yml` (the cost note recomputed; `tests/unit/test_cli_schema_dry_run.py` pins it), `tests/payload/test_recorded_bounds.py` (the new recordings join)

**Interfaces:**

- Produces (U2; every recorder run as `env -u LANGSMITH_API_KEY -u LANGSMITH_TRACING uv run python scripts/spikes/record_anthropic.py --spike <name> --dry-run`, then `--live`; the key in `JOBHUNTER_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; never printed):
  1. **`v4v2`** (the live schema dry-run of prompt-schema version 2; §10.11; R12): the four cases of Task 4's `cases()` for the v2 version on the seeded model policy (generator C on `claude-sonnet-5-5`; critic, profile and queries on `claude-opus-5-5`; effort `low`, `max_tokens` 2,000; the `minimum` probe through `count_tokens`); the recorder commits the four streams under `schema_dry_run/<model>/<schema12>.sse` and the `eval_runs` row of the throwaway database shows `passed = true`, `metrics.transport = "live"`; the case table goes to `docs/spikes/V4.md` ("M3: prompt-schema version 2"). Expected: HTTP 200 and `end_turn` for all four; a generator rejection would be a stop (the plan's premise, V4b, says it compiles on Sonnet). Central ≈ $0.06–0.07 actual; worst ≈ $0.24.
  2. **`v12`** (§22 L6664): `scripts/spikes/v12_batches.py` builds a 3-item batch on `claude-haiku-5-5` with the **real** generator schema C and three synthetic ads (so Haiku's compilation is proven too, A3 §3.7), `custom_id`s `t<n>_a1`, through `BatchesClient.create`; the recorder polls `retrieve` every 60 s up to 24 h (or until `ended`), streams the results, and lists the workspace once (used only to find the batch; an empty listing is never proof of absence, C3); it records the create response, each retrieve, the results JSONL and the listing page (ids and usage only; the item outputs are synthetic). `tests/unit/test_v12_replay.py` replays the recorded results through `process_item` on the fake database path and asserts `usage` present, the `custom_id` pattern, the `created_at` and `request_counts` fields of the listing, and that errored or expired items (if any) settle `not_billed`; an `expired` item cannot be produced on demand (A3 G27), so the expiry decision is covered by Task 17's fake only. Cache behaviour: the batch params keep the marker; `cache_creation_input_tokens` of the first item and `cache_read_input_tokens` of the others are recorded (§21.4's cold-cache assumption). Central < $0.001; worst ≈ $0.012. `docs/spikes/V12.md` holds the result.
  3. **`v5`** with V14 folded in (§22 L6657, L6666; M2 Deviation 9): `scripts/spikes/v5_stream_timing.py` sends 20 streamed calls at effort `medium` through the real gateway: 10 generator calls (`claude-sonnet-5-5`, the C schema) and 10 critic calls (`claude-opus-5-5`), as 5 + 5 **identical-prefix pairs** (the same synthetic rubric block and profile block, a different synthetic ad per pair; the second call of each pair within the 5-minute TTL), one call at a time, each recorded with its SSE event timestamps; it measures per call the duration (sent to settled), the largest gap between SSE events ("pings < 30 s apart during thinking"), `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens`; acceptance: p99 (the maximum of 20) duration < 300 s, every gap < 30 s, the second call of each pair has `cache_read_input_tokens ≥ 4,000` (V14); the first generator call includes the grammar compile (A3 G28: it is reported separately, not excluded). V14's two questions: whether a critic call on Opus/`medium` after a generator pair on Sonnet shows a cache read (no: different model), whether the framing tokens sit in `cache_read` or `input` (the pair's token arithmetic answers it; A3 G7), and, with the optional `v14` pair, whether two schemas on the same (model, effort) share an entry (A3 G6): that pair sends a critic-schema call on Sonnet/`medium` right after a generator call with the same prefix. The recorder refuses the 21st call of `v5`; the stop rule: the script stops at the first refusal and `docs/spikes/V5.md` records what was measured. Central ≈ $0.66, high ≈ $0.83, worst ≈ $4.4 (`v14` + ≈ $0.08 / $0.6). Every recorded stream is committed under `generate/`, `critic/` (and the synthetic streams of Task 4 stay for the shapes they cover); `tests/payload/test_recorded_bounds.py` checks `input_tokens_bound ≥` the recorded usage of each.
  4. The ledger `.fixtures-raw/anthropic-budget.json` after the spikes: every entry done; the settled total copied into `docs/spikes/V19.md`'s spend table (expected about $0.9 of $10.00).

  15. `health`: `GET /api/system/health` has `scoring.pause_reason = null` and `scoring.running_cycle_id = null` at the end; `recompute` of the month is clean (`psql`).

  - `tests/integration/test_m3_end_to_end.py::test_scoring_end_to_end_on_the_fake_llm`, in process with `op_runner`, the keyed fake answers and the fake batches: confirm a profile → `create_targets_for_tuple` → targets with similarity → a `periodic` cycle (`tick_score`) publishes with the full routing (one target with a T5 trigger runs the critic, one with a `revise` outcome revises, one is contested after two revisions) → `det_results` for the active scope → a scope save → the precompute, the final check and the flip with the target moves → `score_now` on a `filtered_out` target with `force` → feedback and a golden case → a 30-case seed (the adversarial eight plus 22 synthetic labels) and a golden eval run on the fake → the gate `passed` → a second confirm → the old tuple cancelled, the bulk operation and a 250-item batch on the fake → `reconciled` with the decision counts → the follow-up cycle → `budget_counters` and `recompute` clean, `bulk_operations.spent_usd ≤ budget_usd` → the retention pass deletes nothing of the active tuple; no log line, checkpoint blob, cycle report or `operations.payload` contains a marker word of an ad or a draft.
  - `tests/crash/test_score_worker_crashes.py` on the real worker (`jobhunter worker` children with `JOBHUNTER_FAKE_LLM=1`, the fixtures and a stalling fake where named): **C9** (`JOBHUNTER_FAULT=gateway.after_dispatch` kills the worker mid-stream: the entry is `ambiguous` after `llm_ledger_unsettled` at the injected deadline, the target is reclaimed by the second worker's takeover, the new attempt rebuilds from the rows, and the zombie's publish, replayed by hand with the old claim, affects 0 rows); **C14** (`JOBHUNTER_FAULT=claims.beat:sleep=200` stalls the heartbeat task during a 400 s stalling call: `stuck_targets` reclaims at 3 min, the new attempt completes, the original call's publish is rejected by the CAS, and the cycle ends `completed`, never `failed_invariant`); **C17** (the zombie worker of M0's harness with a cycle in flight: the old process's domain writes affect 0 rows, its checkpoints stay on its thread, the new attempt completes); **C29** (`JOBHUNTER_FAULT=score.claim.after_claim` kills the worker: the takeover sweep fails the cycle `orphaned`, reclaims its targets, and the next tick starts a new cycle); SIGTERM during a cycle gives `worker_shutdown_complete` with exit 0 and the in-flight entries `ambiguous`.
- [ ] **Step 2: Write the smoke script** as in Produces, importing M2's helpers; `test_smoke_m3_script.py` checks the check list and that it never prints a cookie or a key.
- [ ] **Step 3: Bring up the stack and run everything**

```bash
timeout 60 sh -c 'until docker info >/dev/null 2>&1; do sleep 1; done'
uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check . && uv run mypy && uv run python scripts/check_llm_boundary.py
uv run --group docparse pytest -W error -q && uv run pytest -W error tests/payload -q && uv run pytest -W error tests/contract -q && uv run pytest -W error tests/eval -q
uv run --group docparse pytest -W error -m docparse tests/docparse -q
uv run pytest tests/stack -m stack -q && uv run pytest tests/perf -m perf -q
(cd frontend && npm test && npm run build && npm run lint && npm run typecheck) && scripts/check-openapi-drift.sh
docker compose --env-file deploy/images.lock -f deploy/compose.dev.yaml -f deploy/compose.fixtures.yaml -f deploy/compose.fakellm.yaml down -v
docker compose --env-file deploy/images.lock -f deploy/compose.dev.yaml -f deploy/compose.fixtures.yaml -f deploy/compose.fakellm.yaml up -d --build --wait
uv run python scripts/smoke_m3.py
uv run jobhunter schema-dry-run      # the fake replays the live v2 streams of Task 26: every gating case passes, exit 0
```

Expected: every command exits 0 (the dry-run included, which flips M2's expected failure: the generator case now passes on prompt-schema version 2), and the smoke script prints fifteen `{"check": …, "ok": true}` lines. No paid call runs in this step.
- [ ] **Step 4: Close the spikes and the roadmap.** This step is a gate:
  - `docs/spikes/V4.md` (the v2 section), `V4b-generator-v2.md`, `V5.md` (the paid part), `V12.md`, `V14.md` and `V19.md` (the M3 re-count and the final spend table) hold their results;
  - `.fixtures-raw/anthropic-budget.json` shows every entry done and a settled total below $10.00, copied into `V19.md`, with no key and no content;
  - `bounds.PROMPT_OVERHEAD_TOKENS` holds the re-measured values and `validate.overrun_buffer_bound` of the seeded settings is at most $2.00;
  - the spec is revision 3.5 (Task 25) and its `test_spec_revision` passes.
  Then set the M3 status cell of the roadmap to `complete`, and leave the other rows unchanged.
- [ ] **Step 5: Commit, then the final 4-eyes review**

```bash
git add scripts/smoke_m3.py tests docs/spikes docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-job-hunter-roadmap.md
git commit -m "test(m3): scoring end to end on the fake LLM through the graphs, the batches and the worker; acceptance smoke; mark M3 complete"
```

Then comes the milestone 4-eyes review of the roadmap's quality gate 3: a whole-branch review by Codex GPT-6 Astra (`model_reasoning_effort="high"`, never `xhigh`) and Claude Fable 5.1. Findings are fixed and the review repeated until both approve. Record it in `docs/council/m3-final-review/`, as M2's was.

---

## Deviations

Each deviation stages a spec item across milestones, or settles something the spec leaves open. Entries that change user-visible behaviour say so. Revision 3.5 of the spec (Task 25) carries every one that changes spec text.

1. **Tables staged across milestones** (continuing M0 Deviation 1, M1 Deviation 1 and M2 Deviation 1). M3 creates the 15 tables of §7.11–7.13 that M1 and M2 left, plus `score_cycles` of §7.6, which no milestone had created and which `score_attempts.cycle_id`, §7.16.8 and §7.17 #10 need (A1 issue 1). It closes the five FKs M2 staged, restores the NOT NULL of `active_config.rubric_version_id` and `prompt_schema_version_id` (M0 Deviation 1, M2 Deviation 32), and creates both §7.18 views. Nothing is staged beyond M3. User-visible: no.
2. **DDL beyond the spec text** (Task 1). Three CHECKs the spec leaves out (`transient_failures >= 0`, `spent_usd <= budget_usd`, `repetition BETWEEN 1 AND 3`; A1 issues 4, 12, 29); `llm_spend_ledger.admitted_buffer_usd` so §7.17 #6's row inequality replays after a buffer edit (M2 Carry-forward; A1 issue 28); `jobs.synthetic_eval` and `profile_versions.synthetic_eval` for the adversarial golden-case rows (Deviation 21); and one index on each FK column that a retention or purge delete reaches through its referencing side (A1 issue 41; M1's note 9 precedent), including `det_results_result` for the cascade from `llm_results` that the partial `det_results_current` cannot serve. The `deferred_group_id … ON DELETE SET NULL` of L2230 cannot fire while a `deferred_duplicate` target names the group (the CHECK of L2244); every group deletion first dissolves the group, which moves its deferred targets to `pending` (Task 15), and M5's purges keep that order (A1 issue 5). User-visible: no.
3. **Layout differences from §4.2.** New modules: `scoring/{facts,det_results,steps,routing,targets,cycles,batches,golden,eval_runner,publish,contested,similarity,reconcilers}.py`, `graphs/score/{deps,context,claims,llm_step}.py`, `graphs/collect/report.py`, `dedup/group_rules.py`, `worker/embedding_reconcile.py`, `settings/activate.py`, `api/routes/{targets,golden,alerts}.py`, `llm/schemas/published.py`, `cli_golden.py`, `scripts/spikes/{v12_batches,v5_stream_timing}.py`, `tests/support/{scoring,batches,eval}.py` and `tests/eval/`. `labelling.py` of §4.2 stays M4's. User-visible: no.
4. **The generator output schema is candidate C of spike V4b, shipped as prompt-schema version 2** (U1, R12; M2 Deviations 17 and 33 closed; A3 G1, G2, G34, S1, S2; Task 4). The API rejects revision 3.4's §11.5 schema and candidates A and B ("The compiled grammar is too large"); C compiled on `claude-sonnet-5-5` (`docs/spikes/V4b-generator-v2.md`). The wire schema becomes generic fact items and generic criterion items; the validator normalises it into the §11.4 fact table and the six criteria before V1–V6, so §11.4, §11.7, §11.8, `llm_results.criteria`/`job_facts` and `result_evidence.subject` are unchanged; the internal draft is what `score_drafts` stores and what the critic and the reviser see. `profile_extract` and `propose_queries` stay byte-identical; version 1 keeps its row and hash. `PROMPT_OVERHEAD_TOKENS` is re-measured against the v2 requests (free); the overrun-buffer bound follows and the seeds stay unless it passes $2.00, which stops the task (M2 Deviation 34's rule). The nullable `level` stays an `anyOf` (the SDK refuses a typeless `enum`). §11.5's preamble names the API's documented limits and that `count_tokens` does not compile. User-visible: the output shape of the generator is internal; results look the same.
5. **Seeding and verification of the shipped versions** (Tasks 3, 4, 11, 20, 21; continuing M2 Deviation 32). `seed_defaults` inserts rubric v1 and v2 and prompt-schema versions 1 and 2 on every `migrate`; a NULL pointer (a fresh install) is set to the **newest shipped** version of each; a set pointer is never changed, so an upgraded install (none exists yet, M2 Deviation 34) keeps v1 until the user activates v2 through the route, which requires a passed **live** schema dry-run of that version (M2 Carry-forward 1) and the eval gate. Because scoring on an unverified schema would burn attempts, the cycle trigger pauses with `pause_reason = "schema_unverified"` while the active prompt-schema version has no passed `schema_dry_run` run, unless the worker runs on the fake (dev and tests); production runs `jobhunter schema-dry-run --live` once after install (the runbook entry of Task 27). Compilation is proven per model, so an `llm_tuple` save that moves the generator or reviser to a model without a passed live run for the active version is refused (`schema_dry_run_required`; A3 G30), and the dry-run CLI can run a candidate policy's cases. User-visible: the pause reason in the health section and on `/api/system/spend`, and the 409.
6. **Rubric v2** (R13; A3 G9; Task 3). §11.3's language anchor (L4129) and §11.4's table (L4197) disagreed on a mandatory language with an `unspecified` level that the profile holds; §11.4's deterministic rule wins (B2 or higher `met`, below `uncomparable`), the anchor text follows it, and the changed text is rubric v2 with a new `content_hash`; v1 stays as data. A fresh install seeds v2 active (Deviation 5); an upgraded install activates it through `POST /api/settings/rubric-versions/{id}/activate` (a re-score). User-visible: the rubric block the generator sees; a question for the user below.
7. **Target creation and revival, claim columns and `override_at`** (R4, R8; A1 issues 7–10, 21, 30; A2 R3; Task 14). A revived target starts a fresh life: `charged_attempts`, `transient_failures`, `not_before`, `resume_draft_id`, `refusal_category`, `state_reason`, `lane`, `override` and `override_at` reset, `bulk_operation_id` from the reviver; its guard (the tuple active again, the revision current, the job unmerged and not fenced) is the caller's, in the same transaction. Leaving `in_progress` clears `claim_id`, `claim_epoch` and `claim_heartbeat_at` and keeps `claimed_at`; every edge that sets `override` sets `override_at` (the CHECK of L2247). A job without a `hard_filter_results` row for the active scope gets it computed inline at creation. §7.17 #11 is tested on `state_changed_at` for revived targets. User-visible: a revived target (a reverted profile, an unmerge) may be scored again with four attempts.
8. **A bulk operation completes only when its `create_targets_for_tuple` operation has ended and none of its targets is `pending`, `in_progress` or `in_batch`, and no submission of it is in flight** (R5; A1 issues 18, 19; Task 14). `budget_deferred` and `deferred_duplicate` targets do not hold it open; when they return to `pending` after completion, their reservations carry no `bulk_operation_id` and debit the bucket. User-visible: `/api/system/spend`'s `bulk_operation` ends a little later than the last batch.
9. **Node 9 and I5 use the run's tuple** (R9; A2 R1, R2; B R5; Task 14). A run creates targets for `runs.llm_tuple_id` while it is still the active tuple, re-checked under `active_config FOR SHARE` per chunk; I5 is evaluated against the run's tuple and passes with a recorded reason when that tuple was superseded during the run (the confirm's `create_targets_for_tuple` owns the new tuple). Node 9 cancels superseded revisions' unpublished targets of the run's tuple only. User-visible: no false `failed_invariant` after a mid-run confirm.
10. **`batch_submissions.reserved_usd` and `followup_held_usd` are `numeric(12,6)`** (R7; A1 issue 11; A2 B6): the ledger and bulk amounts they sum are `numeric(12,6)`. User-visible: no.
11. **A set-based transition helper** (R3; A1 issues 13, 14; Task 2). §7.16's "every state change MUST go through `transition(table, id, …)`" is kept for single rows; the atomic claim, the revive upsert, the bulk cancellations, `budget_release`, the sweeps and target-set deletion use `transition_where` over the same registry, validated the same way, and the `assert_transition` trigger checks every row. `batch_submissions` has no reason column in the registry (its `reconcile_report` is JSONB, written by `set_`), a changed-at column only for the states that have one, and the CAS on `state` as its predicate; `batch_items` uses the submission's predicate through the owner lock. User-visible: no.
12. **`score_attempts.outcome` is a registered machine, and the batch outcomes the spec leaves open are mapped** (A1 issue 15; A2 B10; Task 2): `running → ` each of the 13 terminal outcomes; an abandoned submission ends its attempts `cancelled('batch_abandoned')`; a `retry_online` decision ends the batch attempt with the outcome its cause maps to (`transient_error` for `batch_errored | batch_canceled | batch_expired | batch_missing`, `failed_max_tokens` for `batch_max_tokens`, `failed_invalid_output` for `batch_invalid_output`); an `unexpected_stop_reason` item is `failed_invalid_output`; an `ignored_fenced` item whose attempt is still `running` ends it `cancelled('ignored_fenced')`. User-visible: no.
13. **A batch attempt is charged at `submit_started`** (R6; A1 issue 16; A2 B9; Task 7): the step-2 transaction that marks the item entries `dispatched` sets `charged` on every attempt and `charged_attempts + 1` on every target, which §7.11 L2286 defines as "one LLM call was dispatched". A rejected or ambiguous submission therefore counts against the four attempts; an abandoned one does not. User-visible: a target of a rejected batch has one attempt fewer.
14. **`user_job_actions` is M3's, with the merge, unmerge and tombstone wiring** (R1; A1 issue 2; M1 Deviation 7 closed; Task 22). The seen/saved/applied/hidden/note routes stay M4's. User-visible: merged jobs keep their actions; a re-posted hidden ad stays hidden.
15. **The ledger's `bulk_operation_id` is set only on the reservations of batched targets and their funded follow-ups** (A2 B2; Task 7). §6.9.2 L1173 and §6.9.3 L1187/L1193 are read together: a batched target's item and its follow-ups debit `spent_usd` while they fit, and an entry that would not fit is stored without the id and debits the bucket; online targets of the operation always debit the bucket. The budget is fixed at the operation's start even across a month boundary (A2 R5). User-visible: no.
16. **The graph shape the spec leaves implicit** (A2 G1, G2; Task 9). `TargetOutcome` (`target_id`, `attempt_no`, `outcome`, `state`, `lane`, `cost_usd`) and the generalised `merge_outcomes`; the deterministic nodes' timeouts (60/120/120/60 s) beside the LLM nodes' `TimeoutPolicy`; the subgraph's edges as L3546–3564 with Deviation 17's settlements. User-visible: no.
17. **Settlements inside the per-target path** (A2 T1, T2, T3, T5, T6, T8, T9, T11, T12, T15, T17; Tasks 9, 10): `generate` stores the draft with empty validation columns that `validate` fills (the NOT NULL columns); the error exits of L3551 apply to `critic` and `revise`; a structurally invalid revision after the repair publishes `contested` from the last validated draft (R5's "never withheld") while a failed generator still fails; a reclaim's new attempt pins the then-active execution config and evaluation policy (L3491) and an in-process resume keeps the attempt's; the structural repair is per attempt; `llm_calls.round` is the draft's round for `generate`/`revise` and the critic round for `critic`; `logical_call_no = score_attempts.logical_calls + 1`; `InputUnbounded → failed_request('input_unbounded')`, `LLMNotConfigured → transient_error` without a charge, `ModelMismatch → failed_request('model_mismatch')`; a `claim_lost` attempt is ended by whoever changed the predicate (a reclaim writes `claim_lost`, a cancellation `cancelled`) and the coroutine never writes after a lost claim; `fail` raises `llm.cap_reached` under the month lock after the owner row; `load_context` reads the previous attempt's last call (or `batch_max_tokens`) to start enlarged. User-visible: a contested result where revision 3.4 would have failed the target.
18. **Two alert codes §17.6 lacks** (A2 S5, S6; M2 Deviation 6's pattern; Tasks 10, 11): `scoring.publication_invalid` (critical, L4394's "plus a critical alert") and `score_cycle.failed` (critical, §17.1's "cycle failed, critical alert"). M5's catalogue adds them. User-visible: the alerts.
19. **Where the score lane pauses, and how it resumes** (M2 Deviation 11 closed; A2 T16; B R11; Tasks 11, 13, 18, 21). The pause is checked at the cycle trigger (`cycles.pause_reason`), never inside a running cycle: an **unacknowledged** `llm.reservation_bound_violated`, an open `llm.auth_failed` (which also ends with an acknowledgement, since a paused lane makes no scoring try), an open `llm.cap_reached`, a global purge fence, or an unverified schema (Deviation 5). `POST /api/alerts/{id}/ack` is pulled into M3 to lift the first two. User-visible: the pause reason, and the ack route.
20. **Cycle rulings** (A2 G3–G11, G13, A3 (ĉ), A7; B R4; Task 11): `ĉ` averages online attempts with a dispatched call that ended published under the active tuple and policy, with at least 20 (else §21.2's central); a `score_now` or `batch_followup` trigger during a running cycle is served by that cycle's next claim round (the `more` edge, at most 5 rounds; the lane on the target is the record, and the deferred job finds the one-running index taken); `S = 0` for a `score_now` cycle, the bucket-gated `S` for `batch_followup` (its follow-ups are always-include candidates and count against `S` and the bucket, L3600, while their reservations debit the allocation), `S = max(0, …)`; the cycle row's `S`, `ĉ` and `bucket_usd_at_start` are computed at insert; the cycle records the policy active at its start and each attempt pins the one active at its claim (`ĉ` is keyed by the cycle's); the claim transaction starts with `fence_epoch`; `finalize_cycle` resolves `score_cycle.orphaned` and evaluates `scoring.critic_rate_high`; the claim and every sweep that moved a target bump `ranking_state`; the periodic trigger runs in its own fenced transaction after `run_tick` (it needs the month row, which `run_tick`'s `schedule_state` lock would precede). User-visible: no.
21. **Golden cases** (R2; A1 issues 3, 24, 26; A3 G22; Tasks 19, 21). M3 ships `POST /api/jobs/{job_id}/feedback`, `GET` and `DELETE /api/golden-cases` and a `PATCH /api/golden-cases/{id}` `{active?, is_canary?}` (nothing else writes those two fields; at most two canaries); the labelling UI is M4's. `DELETE` deletes the row and its eval rows without the `golden_case_delete` manifest, which needs M5's manifests. A `label` feedback is `source = 'week1'` and a `wrong_score` feedback `'feedback'`. Adversarial cases are committed synthetic fixtures (`tests/eval/fixtures/adversarial`) that `jobhunter golden seed-adversarial` loads as synthetic jobs, revisions, profile versions and scope versions marked `synthetic_eval`, which get no target, no embedding, no card and are retention-exempt while their case is active. User-visible: the routes and the CLI.
22. **`r_first` and `ĉ_followup`** (A2 A9, S7; Task 7): `r_first` is the worst-case first-stage reservation of a generator call at §21.4's component sizes and the measured overhead (a fixed figure, since each ad's bound differs); `ĉ_followup` is the critic and revision parts of §21.2's per-target cost divided by the first-round critic rate ($0.066939 for policy B, the §21.4 figure). User-visible: no.
23. **Routes** (R10; B R15, R18; Task 21). M3 ships the activation routes (with the live dry-run gate and the eval gate; an activation flips the pointer and runs an internal `llm_tuple` save, which records the override and calls the one shared `activate_tuple`), the two version listings (so the id to activate is discoverable), score-now, retry, the manual cycle, batch release, feedback, golden cases, alert acknowledgement and the M3 fields of `/api/system/spend`; `POST /api/duplicate-groups/{group_id}/score-individually` and everything else in §14.2 stay M4's. User-visible: the routes.
24. **Admission rulings** (A2 A1, A2, A4, A5, A6, T18, R7, G10; A1 issue 20; Tasks 12, 13): always-include takes at most `⌊S / 2⌋` similarity slots (the property test L5697 governs L3635's `ceil`; at `S = 1` always-include waits, and Score now bypasses); the claim records `always_include_reason`, and batched targets are identified through `batch_items`, never `lane`; `max_targets_per_cycle` and `min_cycle_targets` (class `budget`) take effect at the next cycle; the `admission` save sweeps immediately in its own transaction; `expired_unscored → pending` clears the reason; the scope flip's `filtered_out → pending` honours `override`; Score now's 409 is a headroom check against `r_first` (the attempt's reservation is the authority); a slot-backed candidate the claim then skips has cost its lane one credit (as L3650 has it). User-visible: at `S = 1` an always-include target waits for `S ≥ 2`.
25. **Deterministic-layer rulings** (A3 G10, G11, G14, G23, G24, G25, G31, G32; Tasks 3, 6): the degree ordering treats a profile with only `other` degrees as `uncomparable` and an empty education list as `none`; `none_required` is no requirement; the fold of `other` names and certification names is `normalize.text.fold`; T4's 60 is the constant `T4_MIN_MID` (mandatory, never a policy field); the cap of 25 and the penalty of 10 are code constants whose change bumps `DET_ALGORITHM_VERSION`, which stays `"2"` for M3 (no `det_results` row predates it); soft penalties are deduplicated per code with the industry preference as the code `industry`, and `industry_match` is `excluded` only from a non-structured fact (a structured one is the filter's; `outside_include` is never produced in v1); `employment_salary` compares the highest stated EUR figure (`annual_eur_max`, else `annual_eur_min`); `location_work_mode` stays `null` when the possible modes disagree. User-visible: the salary rule for minimum-only ads.
26. **Validator rulings** (A3 G3, G12, G13; Task 5): a missing criterion key in C's array is a V2 failure on that criterion (never a parse failure, L4353); V2's quote requirement applies to every asserted fact, keyed by its fact id, by its code for an id-less fact, or by `must_have:<n>` / `nice_to_have:<n>`; the recomputed `experience_seniority` keeps basis `observed` with the seniority and years facts' quotes and becomes `unknown` when both sub-ratings are unknown. User-visible: no.
27. **Batch rulings** (A1 issues 17, 22, 23, 32; A2 R4, R6, B1, B3–B5, B7, B11–B18; Tasks 14, 16, 17): the `in_batch` targets of a superseded tuple are cancelled in the transaction that records the cancel request (so no late item can pass the fence), and the worker's `batch_poll` calls `messages.batches.cancel` afterwards; `canceled` items of that submission are `ignored_fenced`; every item decision but `followup` releases its hold when processed, a `followup` when its resumed attempt ends, and the `bulk_operations` reconciler releases the rest at completion; the submission's owner row is the spend owner and the operation's claim only executes it; `reserve_batch` reserves every item under one month lock; `batch_submit_rejected` settles the calls `error`; `create` runs under a 600 s client deadline; a byte cap of 200 MiB beside `batch_max_items`; "younger than 29 days" counts from `submit_started_at`; a submission "ends" in `reconciled | released | submit_rejected | abandoned`; `batch_poll` and `batch_reconcile_ambiguous` are periodic aux tasks (the reconciler framework has no 5- or 15-minute cadence); missing items' entries move `dispatched → ambiguous`; the workspace is assumed to be this installation's alone; a follow-up re-validates under its own attempt's policy; `batch_min_targets` governs L3708's "≥ 200". User-visible: the batch cancel reaches the provider within five minutes of a confirm.
28. **Reconciler and alert rulings** (A1 issues 27, 29; A2 A8, S4; Task 18): `stuck_targets` reclaims only targets whose attempt is still `running` (a fenced publish's target waits for the purge); `budget_counters` also audits each active bulk operation's `spent_usd` against the ledger; the admission alerts' windows are the trailing 7 days of attempts for `admitted_per_day` and the similarity-lane admissions' current `similarity` for the percentile; `scoring.stuck_target_repaired`'s subject is the hour; §7.17 #4 is read with §8.7's eligibility list. User-visible: no.
29. **Eval rulings** (A1 issue 25; A3 G15, G20, G21, S3; B R9, R14; Tasks 19, 20): `tuple_identity` is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the four tuple ids; Spearman uses average ranks for ties over published cases, `failed` outcomes count against schema validity, filtered and refused cases are excluded from ρ and the pairwise agreement, the test–retest sample is the ten lowest-id active cases, expected-knockout recall is vacuous for week-1 cases; the gate reads the newest completed golden run of the candidate's (rubric, prompt/schema, model policy, evaluation policy), and with 30 or more active cases and no run it is `failed` (a paid run, or `eval_override_ack`, is needed); the canary baseline key is (case, rubric, prompt/schema, model policy, evaluation policy); `projected_critic_rate` is replayed at a policy row's creation and fixed for the row (an identical payload reuses its row and rate); eval runs reuse the scoring steps with an in-memory store and book every call non-scoring with `golden_case_id`; the C35 test helper registers under kind `export`. User-visible: the gate's `failed` state without a run.
30. **Merge and unmerge details** (R1; §8.6.1 steps 1, 3, 4; §8.6.2 steps 4, 5; Task 22): the snapshot holds both action rows and the from-job's non-terminal target ids; the from-job's `in_batch` targets are cancelled individually (`job_merged`) without cancelling the batch; the unmerge's revival goes through `create_or_revive`; the note cut at 20,000 characters keeps both full notes in the snapshot. User-visible: the merged note's separator line.
31. **Retention of the score tables is M3's** (B R13, R17; A2 G12; Task 23): the §20.1 rows for score history (12 months after the terminal end, inactive tuples only, active golden cases' results kept), superseded instances, cycles and bulk operations (13 months) and the `cycle-` checkpoint threads (14 days), with target-set deletion implemented for retention and reused by M5's purges; the roadmap gave §20 to M5, but a running score lane would otherwise grow without bound. User-visible: no.
32. **The Arbeitnow coverage-risk warning uses V8's page yield** (U3; M2 Deviation 39 closed; Task 24): `ARBEITNOW_HOURS_PER_PAGE = 17.5` (page 1 spans about 17.5 h of postings in V8's recordings) replaces the assumed 37; the cap formula stays. User-visible: the warning fires for intervals of about 72 h and more.
33. **`display.timezone` has the effect class `schedule`** (U3; Task 24): a timezone change re-anchors the schedule through the existing `schedule` action, and the dry-run lists it. User-visible: the effect class shown for a timezone change, and the re-anchored schedule.
34. **Spikes** (U2; M2 Deviation 9; A3 G26–G29; Task 26): a fresh live dry-run of version 2 (`v4v2`), V12 on Haiku with the real C schema, V5's 20 calls as identical-prefix pairs with V14 folded in (10 generator + 10 critic), an optional two-call `v14` pair for the same-model two-schema question, each with its own count and the $10.00 actual stop rule; an expired batch item cannot be produced on demand, so that decision is tested on the fake only; V5's p99 of 20 samples is the maximum, with the first call's grammar compile reported separately. Every recorder run unsets `LANGSMITH_API_KEY` for its own process. User-visible: no.
35. **Worker wiring** (B R6, R10, R12, R23; Task 27): four new tasks (`score_cycle` on `score` with `lock="score"`, `batch_poll`, `batch_reconcile_ambiguous` on `aux`, `canary_daily` on `tick`); `defer_operation_job` takes a `lock`; the sweeps run in registration order and none depends on another; the pools keep the spec's sizes and a perf test measures a 60-target cycle; `budget_release` and `stuck_targets` run on the minute tick and the rest on the heavy pass; the `_ADMISSION` split (M2 Carry-forward I3). User-visible: no.
36. **Test harness and fakes** (R11; B R2, R7, R8; Tasks 1, 8, 19): keyed fake answers (`X-JH-Fake-Key`, fake mode only) and a scripted fake Batches API; `reset_database` deletes the M3 tables before the M1 truncate and points the configuration at the newest shipped versions; tests of "ship, then activate" insert their own inactive version; the eval-run test helper moves to kind `export`. User-visible: no.
37. **Cost-model notes** (A3 G6, G7, G8, G16–G19; §21; Task 25): §21.4's sizing is restated at the measured overhead and the v2 schema size; §21.1 names the structured-output framing tokens; §10.6 notes that cache entries are per (model, effort, schema), which V14 measures and which makes preset A's shared entry an estimate; the arithmetic notes of G16–G18 stay as they are (judgements, not errors); the canary cost of §21.3 ($3.80 with cache writes) is the figure the plan uses. User-visible: the projection figures only.

## Questions for the user

Each is a provisional ruling the plan already applies; a different answer changes the named task.

1. **Rubric v2 (R13, Deviation 6).** The anchor fix makes rubric v2; the plan seeds v2 as the active rubric on a fresh install and leaves an upgraded install on v1 until the user activates v2 (a re-score). **Recommendation:** accept; no production database exists, so no re-score is ever paid for the fix.
2. **Always-include at most `⌊S / 2⌋` (Deviation 24).** The property test's "never exceeds half of `S`" wins over `ceil(0.5 × S)`, so at `S = 1` an always-include candidate waits for a larger cycle. **Recommendation:** accept; Score now bypasses admission for anything urgent.
3. **The eval gate without a run (Deviation 29).** With 30 or more active golden cases and no golden run for a candidate, the gate is `failed`, so a rubric, prompt-schema, model-policy or evaluation-policy change needs a paid golden run (about $2.70) or `eval_override_ack`. **Recommendation:** accept; the alternative (`uncalibrated` until a run exists) would let any change bypass the gate.
4. **The unverified-schema pause and the per-model dry-run gate (Deviation 5).** Production scores nothing until `jobhunter schema-dry-run --live` has passed once for the active prompt-schema version, and an `llm_tuple` save that moves the generator to a model never dry-run is refused until a live run covers it. **Recommendation:** accept; compilation is proven per model and a rejected run is free.
5. **`DELETE /api/golden-cases/{id}` without its manifest, and the `PATCH` route (Deviation 21).** The delete lands in M3 without the `golden_case_delete` manifest (M5's manifests), and `PATCH {active?, is_canary?}` is the only writer of those fields. **Recommendation:** accept; the manifest is added in M5 with the other deletion manifests.
